{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"call_TKWrwIEQcyv4zBl80YJjEcHp","type":"tool_result","content":"Web search results for query: \"HWPX mimetype file content application/vnd.hancom.hwpx\"\n\nHere are the most relevant web results for **“HWPX mimetype file content application/vnd.hancom.hwpx”**, plus what they collectively indicate about **(a)** the *internal* `mimetype` file inside an `.hwpx` ZIP package vs **(b)** the *external* MIME type often used for HTTP uploads/downloads.\n\n## What the web results say (key findings)\n\n### 1) HWPX contains a top-level `mimetype` file used as a format signature\nHancom’s own technical blog describing the **HWPX package structure** explains that an HWPX file is **ZIP-based** and that the **`mimetype` file** is used as **signature information** to determine whether the package is truly HWPX. ([tech.hancom.com](https://tech.hancom.com/hwpxformat/))\n\n### 2) Multiple sources say the internal `mimetype` file content is `application/hwp+zip` (not `application/vnd.hancom.hwpx`)\nAn official-looking answer on **Hancom Developer Forum** (a Hancom-run forum) states:\n\n- For **HWPX**, detection can be done by checking the top-level `mimetype` file content, and that value is **`application/hwp+zip`**. ([forum.developer.hancom.com](https://forum.developer.hancom.com/t/hwp-hwpx-mime-type-whitelist/1641))\n\nThis same “`application/hwp+zip`” identification is also echoed by:\n- **Apache Tika’s** MIME-type entry for `application/hwp+zip` (described as “Hangul Word Processor File, zip based”, default extension `.hwpx`). ([ciam-tika.straumann.com](https://ciam-tika.straumann.com/mime-types/application/hwp%2Bzip?utm_source=openai))  \n- A **Pandoc discussion** proposing an HWPX writer, which shows an example HWPX ZIP layout and explicitly lists the `mimetype` file as `application/hwp+zip`. ([github.com](https://github.com/jgm/pandoc/discussions/11569?utm_source=openai))  \n- A GitHub wiki page describing HWPX internal structure, also stating that the `mimetype` file records `application/hwp+zip`. ([github-wiki-see.page](https://github-wiki-see.page/m/pai-plznw4me/hwp-format-transfer/wiki/hwpx-%EB%82%B4%EB%B6%80%EA%B5%AC%EC%A1%B0?utm_source=openai))  \n\nSo, across several independent references, the internal container “magic” appears to be:\n\n- **`mimetype` file inside `.hwpx`**: `application/hwp+zip` ([forum.developer.hancom.com](https://forum.developer.hancom.com/t/hwp-hwpx-mime-type-whitelist/1641))\n\n### 3) `application/vnd.hancom.hwpx` is widely used as an *external* MIME type mapping for `.hwpx`\nEven though the internal `mimetype` file is often described as `application/hwp+zip`, other sources show that `.hwpx` is commonly mapped to the vendor MIME type:\n\n- A long-running **hwp-foss Google Groups** thread listing MIME types includes:  \n  `*.hwpx - application/vnd.hancom.hwpx` ([groups.google.com](https://groups.google.com/g/hwp-foss/c/64d9HQj3GQA?utm_source=openai))\n- Blog posts about configuring web servers (e.g., Apache) show adding:  \n  `AddType application/vnd.hancom.hwpx .hwpx` ([blueyikim.tistory.com](https://blueyikim.tistory.com/434?category=131501&utm_source=openai))\n\nSo, across various environments, you will encounter:\n\n- **HTTP / upload / extension-based mapping**: `application/vnd.hancom.hwpx` ([groups.google.com](https://groups.google.com/g/hwp-foss/c/64d9HQj3GQA?utm_source=openai))\n\n### 4) Hancom forum explicitly discusses that HWPX doesn’t have a universally “officially registered” HTTP Content-Type\nA related Hancom Developer Forum thread about HTTP `Content-Type` says (paraphrasing): HWPX isn’t “officially registered” as a MIME type; you can use `application/hwp+zip` if you’ve implemented a custom agreement/behavior on the client side, otherwise `application/octet-stream` can be used since it’s ZIP binary. Paragraph text is represented as multiple <hp:t> nodes.\n    parts: List[str] = []\n    for t in p.iter(f\"{{{HWPML_NS}}}t\"):\n        parts.append(t.text or \"\")\n    return \"\".join(parts)\n\n\ndef _first_run_with_text(p: ET.Element) -> Optional[ET.Element]:\n    for run in p.findall(\"hp:run\", NS):\n        if run.find(\"hp:t\", NS) is not None:\n            return run\n    return None\n\n\ndef collapse_paragraph_to_single_run(p: ET.Element, new_text: str) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Collapse paragraph runs into one run with one <hp:t>.\n\n    Preserves the first run's attributes (e.g., charPrIDRef).\n    Leaves other non-run children (like <hp:linesegarray>) untouched.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    runs = p.findall(\"hp:run\", NS)\n    first_run = _first_run_with_text(p)\n\n    if first_run is None:\n        # Create a run at the start if none exist.\n        first_run = ET.Element(f\"{{{HWPML_NS}}}run\")\n        if runs:\n            # Insert before the first existing run.\n            idx = list(p).index(runs[0])\n            p.insert(idx, first_run)\n        else:\n            # Insert at paragraph start.\n            p.insert(0, first_run)\n        runs = p.findall(\"hp:run\", NS)\n\n    # Remove all children of first run (including existing <hp:t> nodes), then\n    # insert a single <hp:t>.\n    for child in list(first_run):\n        first_run.remove(child)\n    t = ET.SubElement(first_run, f\"{{{HWPML_NS}}}t\")\n    t.text = new_text\n\n    # Remove all other runs that contain text. We only remove runs, not other\n    # paragraph children.\n    for run in runs:\n        if run is first_run:\n            continue\n        # If it had <hp:t>, remove it; otherwise leave (could be non-text run).\n        if run.find(\"hp:t\", NS) is not None:\n            p.remove(run)\n\n\ndef consolidate_split_placeholders(p: ET.Element) -> bool:\n    \"\"\"If a paragraph contains a placeholder marker, collapse its runs.\n\n    Returns True if a structural consolidation happened.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    joined = paragraph_text(p)\n    if \"{{\" not in joined:\n        return False\n\n    #
